<a id="pm-s1"></a>
## 1. Abstract and result contract

> Every convex body in ℝ<sup><i>n</i></sup>, <i>n</i> ≥ 2, admits a covering by translates along one full-rank lattice with density at most $`Cn\log n`$, for an absolute constant <i>C</i>. No symmetry or boundary regularity is assumed.

- **C1** (theorem, [source](#stmt-thm-main)): There is an absolute constant $C>0$ such that, for every integer $n\ge2$ and every convex body $K\subset\mathbb R^n$, one full-rank lattice $\Lambda$ satisfies $$K+\Lambda=\mathbb R^n,\qquad \frac{\operatorname{vol}_n(K)}{\det\Lambda}\le Cn\log n.$$ No symmetry or boundary regularity is assumed.
  - Explanation: Every convex body admits a single-lattice covering with the stated density bound, without symmetry or boundary regularity.
- **C2** (construction, [source](#stmt-lem-sections)): There are absolute constants $c,C>0$ with the following property. Fix $M\ge1$. For all sufficiently large $n$, with threshold depending only on $M$, suppose that $D$ is a positive integer and $$1\le D,R\le M(1+\log\log n)^M,\qquad m=n-D.$$ Every convex body in $\mathbb R^n$ has an invertible affine image $K\subset\mathbb R^m\times\mathbb R^D$ and a list of at most $(Cm^2)^D$ convex bodies $J_a\subset\mathbb R^m$ such that, writing $K_y=\{x:(x,y)\in K\}$, for every $\\|y\\|\le R$ some label $a$ satisfies $$J_a\subset K_y, \qquad c\,\operatorname{vol}_n(K)\gamma_D(y) \le \operatorname{vol}_m(J_a) \le C\,\operatorname{vol}_n(K)\gamma_D(y). \label{eq:section-labels}$$
  - Explanation: Affine normalization provides finitely many horizontal bodies contained in nearby sections, with volumes comparable to the Gaussian marginal.
- **C3** (conditional_result, [source](#stmt-lem-horizontal)): Let $J_1,\ldots,J_N$ be convex bodies in $\mathbb R^m$, let $D_h>0$, and set $u_a=\operatorname{vol}_m(J_a)/D_h$. If $$\max_a u_a\le\eta_m, \qquad (1+C_{\mathrm R})\sum_{a=1}^N e^{-u_a/2}<1, \label{eq:horizontal-criterion}$$ there is a lattice $\Lambda_h$ of determinant $D_h$ satisfying $$h_{\Lambda_h}(J_a)\le e^{-u_a/2} \qquad (1\le a\le N). \label{eq:horizontal-holes}$$ In particular, this conclusion holds for all sufficiently large $m$ if $$\min_a u_a\ge\sqrt m,\qquad \max_a u_a=o(m),\qquad \log N=o(\sqrt m).$$ The dimension threshold in this last formulation depends only on the three numerical bounds, not on the shapes of the bodies.
  - Explanation: The volume and summability conditions provide a common horizontal lattice with exponentially small holes for every listed body.
- **C4** (bound, [source](#stmt-lem-sampler)): There is an absolute constant $b_0$ with the following property. Let $b\ge b_0$, let $H$ be a finite abelian group written additively, and suppose that $$\log \|H\|\le b^4, \qquad 0\le g\le \exp(b^{0.65}), \qquad \bar g:=\mathbb E_{x\in H}g(x)\in[1/2,2].$$ Let $s$ be an integer satisfying $b^{0.69}\le s\le b$, and choose $w_1,\ldots,w_s$ independently and uniformly in $H$. With $c_*=(\log 2)/8$, the probability that $$\label{eq:sampler-conclusion} 2^{-s}\sum_{\varepsilon\in\{0,1\}^s} g\left(x-\sum_{i=1}^s\varepsilon_iw_i\right) \ge (1-3b^{-1/10})\bar g \qquad\text{for every }x\in H$$ is at least $$\label{eq:sampler-probability} 1-\exp(-c_*b^{0.69}) -b\exp\left(-\frac{c_*}{2}b^{0.59}\right).$$ In particular, the failure probability is at most $b^{-1/2}$ after increasing $b_0$.
  - Explanation: Random Boolean subset sums provide a lower average uniformly over all base points, with the stated failure bound.
- **C5** (construction, [source](#stmt-lem-folded-grid)): For every sufficiently large $b$, there is a prime $p_b$ satisfying $$b\mu_b-4b^{0.56}\le\log p_b\le b\mu_b-3b^{0.56}. \label{eq:block-prime}$$ Let $G_b=\mathbb F_{p_b}^b$, and associate with $z\in G_b$ the anchor $t_z=z/p_b\in[0,1)^b$, using the representatives $0,\ldots,p_b-1$ in every coordinate. Define the eligible set $E_b\subset G_b$ by the two conditions $$\left\|\log F_b(t_z)-b\mu_b\right\|\le b^{0.56},\qquad \#\left\{j:\left\|t_{z,j}-\frac12\right\|\le h_b^{-2}\right\} \ge b^{0.70}. \label{eq:block-eligible}$$ Then $$\mathbb E_{z\in G_b}F_b(t_z)=1+O(b^{-1/20}),\qquad \mathbb E_{z\in G_b}F_b(t_z)\mathbf 1_{E_b}(z)=1+O(b^{-1/20}). \label{eq:block-mass}$$ Moreover, $\mu_b=\log h_b+O(1)$. Put $\eta_b=bh_b^2/p_b$. If $t$ belongs to the grid cell $Q_z=t_z+[0,p_b^{-1})^b$, then, for every $\varepsilon\in\{0,1\}^b$, $$\begin{split} e^{-\eta_b}F_b(t_z)&\le F_b(t)\le e^{\eta_b}F_b(t_z),\\ e^{-\eta_b}\frac{F_b(t_z)}{p_b}P_{b,t_z}(\varepsilon) &\le\frac{h_b^b}{p_b}\gamma_b\bigl(h_b(t-\varepsilon)\bigr) \le e^{\eta_b}\frac{F_b(t_z)}{p_b}P_{b,t_z}(\varepsilon). \end{split} \label{eq:block-rounding}$$ There is an absolute $c>0$ such that, for every eligible $z$, every $t\in Q_z$, and every $\varepsilon\in\{0,1\}^b$, $$\frac{F_b(t_z)}{p_b}P_{b,t_z}(\varepsilon)\le e^{-c b^{0.70}}, \qquad \frac{h_b^b}{p_b}\gamma_b\bigl(h_b(t-\varepsilon)\bigr) \le e^{-c b^{0.70}}. \label{eq:block-atom}$$
  - Explanation: A prime grid retains almost all folded Gaussian mass on eligible anchors while bounding each individual atom.
- **C6** (construction, [source](#stmt-lem-usable)): There are absolute constants $A,B>0$ with the following property. Let $b\ge B$ be an integer, and use the objects in Lemma <a href="#lem:folded-grid" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="lem:folded-grid">6</a>. Suppose that $H$ is a finite abelian group, $\log\|H\|\le b^4$, and $g_0:H\to[0,\infty)$ satisfies $$\frac12\le\bar g_0:=\mathbb E_{x\in H}g_0(x)\le2,\qquad g_0(x)\le\exp(5a^{0.56})\quad(x\in H)$$ for some $1\le a\le b^{1/0.9}$. Then one can choose shifts $w_1,\ldots,w_b\in H$, a usable set $U_b\subset E_b$, and a one-dimensional linear subspace $C_b\subset G_b$ such that, writing $W\varepsilon=\sum_{j=1}^b\varepsilon_jw_j$, $$\sum_{\varepsilon\in\{0,1\}^b}P_{b,t_z}(\varepsilon)g_0(x-W\varepsilon) \ge(1-A b^{-1/100})\bar g_0 \qquad(z\in U_b,\ x\in H). \label{eq:block-biased-sampler}$$ Define $$v_b(z)=\frac{F_b(t_z)}{p_b}\mathbf 1_{U_b}(z),\qquad g_b(x)=\max_{c\in C_b}v_b(x-c),\qquad \bar g_b=\mathbb E_{x\in G_b}g_b(x). \label{eq:block-line-weight}$$ These choices satisfy $$\|\bar g_b-1\|\le A b^{-1/100},\qquad 0\le g_b(x)\le\exp(5b^{0.56})\quad(x\in G_b),\qquad \left\|\mathbb E_z F_b(t_z)\mathbf 1_{U_b}(z)-1\right\| \le A b^{-1/20}. \label{eq:block-usable-bounds}$$ There is also a base version with no $H$, $g_0$, or $W$: take $U_b=E_b$ and choose $C_b$ so that the conclusions concerning $g_b$ in Equation <a href="#eq:block-usable-bounds" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:block-usable-bounds">[eq:block-usable-bounds]</a> hold.
  - Explanation: Usable anchors sample a preceding mean uniformly, and a line coset maximum retains almost all weighted mass. The maximum need not be positive in every coset.
- **C7** (conditional_result, [source](#stmt-lem-block-cell)): Let $L_b=\mathbb Z^b+C_b/p_b$. For $t\in\mathbb R^b$ put $k=\lfloor p_b t\rfloor\in\mathbb Z^b$ and $x=k\pmod{p_b}\in G_b$. Floors and fractional parts are taken coordinatewise. If $g_b(x)>0$, choose $c\in C_b$ attaining its maximum and let $z=x-c\in U_b$, again using standard representatives. Then $$\lambda=\frac{k-z}{p_b}\in L_b,\qquad \tau=t-\lambda=\frac{z+\{p_b t\}}{p_b}\in Q_z,\qquad g_b(x)=\frac{F_b(t_z)}{p_b}. \label{eq:block-cell-identity}$$ For all $\varepsilon\in\{0,1\}^b$, the point $\lambda+\varepsilon$ lies in $L_b$, its residual $t-\lambda-\varepsilon=\tau-\varepsilon$ has coordinates in $[-1,1]$, and $$\frac{h_b^b}{p_b}\gamma_b\bigl(h_b(t-\lambda-\varepsilon)\bigr) \ge e^{-\eta_b}g_b(x)P_{b,t_z}(\varepsilon). \label{eq:block-cell-lower}$$ The corresponding individual weight is at most $e^{-c b^{0.70}}$.
  - Explanation: A maximizing usable anchor produces lattice points with bounded residuals and the stated Gaussian weight comparisons.
- **C8** (construction, [source](#stmt-prop-vertical)): There are absolute constants $B,C,c,\kappa>0$ such that, for every integer $b_1\ge B$, the construction above has $$D\le C(b_1+1),\qquad R^2:=\sum_{i=1}^{r+1}b_i h_i^2 \le C\bigl(b_1\log(b_1+1)+1\bigr). \label{eq:hierarchy-dimension-radius}$$ For every $y\in\mathbb R^D$ there is a finite nonempty pattern $\mathcal P_y\subset L$ such that $$\begin{aligned} \\|(y-M\ell)_i\\|_\infty&\le h_i \qquad(\ell\in\mathcal P_y,\ 1\le i\le r+1), \label{eq:hierarchy-residual}\\ \sum_{\ell\in\mathcal P_y}D_v\gamma_D(y-M\ell)&\ge\kappa, \label{eq:hierarchy-total}\\ \max_{\ell\in\mathcal P_y}D_v\gamma_D(y-M\ell) &\le \exp(-c b_1^{0.70}). \label{eq:hierarchy-cap} \end{aligned}$$ The terminal block is constant on the pattern. For each ordinary block $i$ and each fixed suffix $(\ell_{i+1},\ldots,\ell_{r+1})$, its retained values belong to $\lambda_i+\{0,1\}^{b_i}$ for one $\lambda_i\in L_i$ depending on that suffix. In particular $\|\mathcal P_y\|\le 2^D$.* *Order the scalar coordinates by increasing block index and denote them by $\ell[1],\ldots,\ell[D]$. Conditional on any fixed scalar suffix $\ell[j+1],\ldots,\ell[D]$, the possible next values $\ell[j]$ are at most two; if there are two, they differ by $1$. This property is preserved upon deleting any subset of the pattern.* *If $y\in M[0,1)^D$, all raw coordinates can in addition be restricted to the fixed finite alphabets $$\begin{aligned} \ell_i&\in\bigl(p_i^{-1}\mathbb Z\cap[-Q_{\rm raw},Q_{\rm raw}]\bigr)^{b_i} &&(i\le r),\\ \ell_{r+1}&\in\bigl(\mathbb Z\cap[-Q_{\rm raw},Q_{\rm raw}]\bigr)^T, &&Q_{\rm raw}=1+(D+2)^D. \end{aligned} \label{eq:hierarchy-alphabets}$$ For an ordinary block the logarithm of each scalar alphabet size is $O(D\log(D+2)+b_i\log b_i)$; the terminal version omits the second term. All implicit constants are absolute.
  - Explanation: A weighted branching hierarchy has uniformly positive total mass, small individual atoms, and suffix-dependent binary choices in finite coordinate alphabets.
- **C9** (conditional_result, [source](#stmt-lem-shear)): Let $\mathcal T=\mathbb R^m/\Lambda_h$, and let $U_a\subset\mathcal T$ be measurable sets with $\|U_a\|\le\eta_a$, where $\eta_a>0$. For $0\le q\le D$, let $\mathcal P_q$ be finite families of labeled patterns in $\mathbb R^q$, binary by suffixes. Suppose they are closed under last-coordinate slicing. Write $$Q=\sum_{q=0}^D\|\mathcal P_q\|,\qquad L_*>\max(1,Q).$$ There are real vectors $Z_1,\ldots,Z_D\in\mathbb R^m$ such that, for every $P\in\mathcal P_q$, $$\label{eq:shear-bound} \left\|\bigcap_{l\in P} \left(U_{a(l)}+ \pi_{\Lambda_h}\left(\sum_{j=1}^q l[j]Z_j\right)\right)\right\| \le L_*^{\|P\|-1}\prod_{l\in P}\eta_{a(l)}.$$ Here $l[j]$ denotes the $j$th scalar coordinate of $l$.
  - Explanation: A common linear shear bounds all shifted hole intersections in a finite family closed under slicing.
- **C10** (conditional_result, [source](#stmt-lem-completion)): Let $K\subset\mathbb R^n$ be a convex body and $\Gamma$ a full-rank lattice. If its hole proportion $\delta=1-\|\pi_\Gamma(K)\|$ satisfies $$\delta<(1-\delta)k^{-n}$$ for a positive integer $k$, then $(1+1/k)K+\Gamma=\mathbb R^n$.
  - Explanation: A sufficiently small torus hole proportion yields exact coverage after the prescribed dilation.

Let $b\\ge b_0$, let $H$ be a finite abelian group written additively, and suppose that $$\\log |H|\\le b^4,\n  \\qquad 0\\le g\\le \\exp(b^{0.65}),\n  \\qquad \\bar g:=\\mathbb E_{x\\in H}g(x)\\in[1/2,2].$$ Let $s$ be an integer satisfying $b^{0.69}\\le s\\le b$, and choose $w_1,\\ldots,w_s$ independently and uniformly in $H$. With $c_*=(\\log 2)/8$, the probability that $$\\label{eq:sampler-conclusion}\n  2^{-s}\\sum_{\\varepsilon\\in\\{0,1\\}^s}\n  g\\left(x-\\sum_{i=1}^s\\varepsilon_iw_i\\right)\n  \\ge (1-3b^{-1/10})\\bar g\n  \\qquad\\text{for every }x\\in H$$ is at least $$\\label{eq:sampler-probability}\n  1-\\exp(-c_*b^{0.69})\n    -b\\exp\\left(-\\frac{c_*}{2}b^{0.59}\\right).$$ In particular, the failure probability is at most $b^{-1/2}$ after increasing $b_0$.",

      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-lem-folded-grid",
        "exact_quote": "For every sufficiently large $b$, there is a prime $p_b$ satisfying $$b\\mu_b-4b^{0.56}\\le\\log p_b\\le b\\mu_b-3b^{0.56}.\n \\label{eq:block-prime}$$ Let $G_b=\\mathbb F_{p_b}^b$, and associate with $z\\in G_b$ the anchor $t_z=z/p_b\\in[0,1)^b$, using the representatives $0,\\ldots,p_b-1$ in every coordinate. Define the eligible set $E_b\\subset G_b$ by the two conditions $$\\left|\\log F_b(t_z)-b\\mu_b\\right|\\le b^{0.56},\\qquad\n \\#\\left\\{j:\\left|t_{z,j}-\\frac12\\right|\\le h_b^{-2}\\right\\}\n       \\ge b^{0.70}.\n \\label{eq:block-eligible}$$ Then $$\\mathbb E_{z\\in G_b}F_b(t_z)=1+O(b^{-1/20}),\\qquad\n \\mathbb E_{z\\in G_b}F_b(t_z)\\mathbf 1_{E_b}(z)=1+O(b^{-1/20}).\n \\label{eq:block-mass}$$ Moreover, $\\mu_b=\\log h_b+O(1)$. Put $\\eta_b=bh_b^2/p_b$. If $t$ belongs to the grid cell $Q_z=t_z+[0,p_b^{-1})^b$, then, for every $\\varepsilon\\in\\{0,1\\}^b$, $$\\begin{split}\n e^{-\\eta_b}F_b(t_z)&\\le F_b(t)\\le e^{\\eta_b}F_b(t_z),\\\\\n e^{-\\eta_b}\\frac{F_b(t_z)}{p_b}P_{b,t_z}(\\varepsilon)\n &\\le\\frac{h_b^b}{p_b}\\gamma_b\\bigl(h_b(t-\\varepsilon)\\bigr)\n \\le e^{\\eta_b}\\frac{F_b(t_z)}{p_b}P_{b,t_z}(\\varepsilon).\n \\end{split}\n \\label{eq:block-rounding}$$ There is an absolute $c>0$ such that, for every eligible $z$, every $t\\in Q_z$, and every $\\varepsilon\\in\\{0,1\\}^b$, $$\\frac{F_b(t_z)}{p_b}P_{b,t_z}(\\varepsilon)\\le e^{-c b^{0.70}},\n \\qquad\n \\frac{h_b^b}{p_b}\\gamma_b\\bigl(h_b(t-\\varepsilon)\\bigr)\n       \\le e^{-c b^{0.70}}.\n \\label{eq:block-atom}$$",
        "source_id": "PM-092.1"
      },
      "evidence": [
        {
          "anchor": "stmt-lem-folded-grid",

      "exact_statement": "For every sufficiently large $b$, there is a prime $p_b$ satisfying $$b\\mu_b-4b^{0.56}\\le\\log p_b\\le b\\mu_b-3b^{0.56}.\n \\label{eq:block-prime}$$ Let $G_b=\\mathbb F_{p_b}^b$, and associate with $z\\in G_b$ the anchor $t_z=z/p_b\\in[0,1)^b$, using the representatives $0,\\ldots,p_b-1$ in every coordinate. Define the eligible set $E_b\\subset G_b$ by the two conditions $$\\left|\\log F_b(t_z)-b\\mu_b\\right|\\le b^{0.56},\\qquad\n \\#\\left\\{j:\\left|t_{z,j}-\\frac12\\right|\\le h_b^{-2}\\right\\}\n       \\ge b^{0.70}.\n \\label{eq:block-eligible}$$ Then $$\\mathbb E_{z\\in G_b}F_b(t_z)=1+O(b^{-1/20}),\\qquad\n \\mathbb E_{z\\in G_b}F_b(t_z)\\mathbf 1_{E_b}(z)=1+O(b^{-1/20}).\n \\label{eq:block-mass}$$ Moreover, $\\mu_b=\\log h_b+O(1)$. Put $\\eta_b=bh_b^2/p_b$. If $t$ belongs to the grid cell $Q_z=t_z+[0,p_b^{-1})^b$, then, for every $\\varepsilon\\in\\{0,1\\}^b$, $$\\begin{split}\n e^{-\\eta_b}F_b(t_z)&\\le F_b(t)\\le e^{\\eta_b}F_b(t_z),\\\\\n e^{-\\eta_b}\\frac{F_b(t_z)}{p_b}P_{b,t_z}(\\varepsilon)\n &\\le\\frac{h_b^b}{p_b}\\gamma_b\\bigl(h_b(t-\\varepsilon)\\bigr)\n \\le e^{\\eta_b}\\frac{F_b(t_z)}{p_b}P_{b,t_z}(\\varepsilon).\n \\end{split}\n \\label{eq:block-rounding}$$ There is an absolute $c>0$ such that, for every eligible $z$, every $t\\in Q_z$, and every $\\varepsilon\\in\\{0,1\\}^b$, $$\\frac{F_b(t_z)}{p_b}P_{b,t_z}(\\varepsilon)\\le e^{-c b^{0.70}},\n \\qquad\n \\frac{h_b^b}{p_b}\\gamma_b\\bigl(h_b(t-\\varepsilon)\\bigr)\n       \\le e^{-c b^{0.70}}.\n \\label{eq:block-atom}$$",
      "explanation": "A prime grid retains almost all folded Gaussian mass on eligible anchors while bounding each individual atom.",
      "fidelity": "PENDING",
      "hypotheses": [
        {
          "anchor": "stmt-lem-folded-grid",
          "exact_quote": "For every sufficiently large $b$, there is a prime $p_b$ satisfying $$b\\mu_b-4b^{0.56}\\le\\log p_b\\le b\\mu_b-3b^{0.56}.\n \\label{eq:block-prime}$$ Let $G_b=\\mathbb F_{p_b}^b$, and associate with $z\\in G_b$ the anchor $t_z=z/p_b\\in[0,1)^b$, using the representatives $0,\\ldots,p_b-1$ in every coordinate. Define the eligible set $E_b\\subset G_b$ by the two conditions $$\\left|\\log F_b(t_z)-b\\mu_b\\right|\\le b^{0.56},\\qquad\n \\#\\left\\{j:\\left|t_{z,j}-\\frac12\\right|\\le h_b^{-2}\\right\\}\n       \\ge b^{0.70}.\n \\label{eq:block-eligible}$$ Then $$\\mathbb E_{z\\in G_b}F_b(t_z)=1+O(b^{-1/20}),\\qquad\n \\mathbb E_{z\\in G_b}F_b(t_z)\\mathbf 1_{E_b}(z)=1+O(b^{-1/20}).\n \\label{eq:block-mass}$$ Moreover, $\\mu_b=\\log h_b+O(1)$. Put $\\eta_b=bh_b^2/p_b$. If $t$ belongs to the grid cell $Q_z=t_z+[0,p_b^{-1})^b$, then, for every $\\varepsilon\\in\\{0,1\\}^b$, $$\\begin{split}\n e^{-\\eta_b}F_b(t_z)&\\le F_b(t)\\le e^{\\eta_b}F_b(t_z),\\\\\n e^{-\\eta_b}\\frac{F_b(t_z)}{p_b}P_{b,t_z}(\\varepsilon)\n &\\le\\frac{h_b^b}{p_b}\\gamma_b\\bigl(h_b(t-\\varepsilon)\\bigr)\n \\le e^{\\eta_b}\\frac{F_b(t_z)}{p_b}P_{b,t_z}(\\varepsilon).\n \\end{split}\n \\label{eq:block-rounding}$$ There is an absolute $c>0$ such that, for every eligible $z$, every $t\\in Q_z$, and every $\\varepsilon\\in\\{0,1\\}^b$, $$\\frac{F_b(t_z)}{p_b}P_{b,t_z}(\\varepsilon)\\le e^{-c b^{0.70}},\n \\qquad\n \\frac{h_b^b}{p_b}\\gamma_b\\bigl(h_b(t-\\varepsilon)\\bigr)\n       \\le e^{-c b^{0.70}}.\n \\label{eq:block-atom}$$",
          "source_id": "PM-092.1"
        }

Define the eligible set $E_b\\subset G_b$ by the two conditions $$\\left|\\log F_b(t_z)-b\\mu_b\\right|\\le b^{0.56},\\qquad\n \\#\\left\\{j:\\left|t_{z,j}-\\frac12\\right|\\le h_b^{-2}\\right\\}\n       \\ge b^{0.70}.\n \\label{eq:block-eligible}$$ Then $$\\mathbb E_{z\\in G_b}F_b(t_z)=1+O(b^{-1/20}),\\qquad\n \\mathbb E_{z\\in G_b}F_b(t_z)\\mathbf 1_{E_b}(z)=1+O(b^{-1/20}).\n \\label{eq:block-mass}$$ Moreover, $\\mu_b=\\log h_b+O(1)$. Put $\\eta_b=bh_b^2/p_b$. If $t$ belongs to the grid cell $Q_z=t_z+[0,p_b^{-1})^b$, then, for every $\\varepsilon\\in\\{0,1\\}^b$, $$\\begin{split}\n e^{-\\eta_b}F_b(t_z)&\\le F_b(t)\\le e^{\\eta_b}F_b(t_z),\\\\\n e^{-\\eta_b}\\frac{F_b(t_z)}{p_b}P_{b,t_z}(\\varepsilon)\n &\\le\\frac{h_b^b}{p_b}\\gamma_b\\bigl(h_b(t-\\varepsilon)\\bigr)\n \\le e^{\\eta_b}\\frac{F_b(t_z)}{p_b}P_{b,t_z}(\\varepsilon).\n \\end{split}\n \\label{eq:block-rounding}$$ There is an absolute $c>0$ such that, for every eligible $z$, every $t\\in Q_z$, and every $\\varepsilon\\in\\{0,1\\}^b$, $$\\frac{F_b(t_z)}{p_b}P_{b,t_z}(\\varepsilon)\\le e^{-c b^{0.70}},\n \\qquad\n \\frac{h_b^b}{p_b}\\gamma_b\\bigl(h_b(t-\\varepsilon)\\bigr)\n       \\le e^{-c b^{0.70}}.\n \\label{eq:block-atom}$$",

    {
      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-lem-usable",
        "exact_quote": "There are absolute constants $A,B>0$ with the following property. Let $b\\ge B$ be an integer, and use the objects in Lemma <a href=\"#lem:folded-grid\" data-reference-type=\"ref\" data-reference=\"lem:folded-grid\">6</a>. Suppose that $H$ is a finite abelian group, $\\log|H|\\le b^4$, and $g_0:H\\to[0,\\infty)$ satisfies $$\\frac12\\le\\bar g_0:=\\mathbb E_{x\\in H}g_0(x)\\le2,\\qquad\n g_0(x)\\le\\exp(5a^{0.56})\\quad(x\\in H)$$ for some $1\\le a\\le b^{1/0.9}$. Then one can choose shifts $w_1,\\ldots,w_b\\in H$, a usable set $U_b\\subset E_b$, and a one-dimensional linear subspace $C_b\\subset G_b$ such that, writing $W\\varepsilon=\\sum_{j=1}^b\\varepsilon_jw_j$, $$\\sum_{\\varepsilon\\in\\{0,1\\}^b}P_{b,t_z}(\\varepsilon)g_0(x-W\\varepsilon)\n       \\ge(1-A b^{-1/100})\\bar g_0\n       \\qquad(z\\in U_b,\\ x\\in H).\n \\label{eq:block-biased-sampler}$$ Define $$v_b(z)=\\frac{F_b(t_z)}{p_b}\\mathbf 1_{U_b}(z),\\qquad\n g_b(x)=\\max_{c\\in C_b}v_b(x-c),\\qquad\n \\bar g_b=\\mathbb E_{x\\in G_b}g_b(x).\n \\label{eq:block-line-weight}$$ These choices satisfy $$|\\bar g_b-1|\\le A b^{-1/100},\\qquad\n 0\\le g_b(x)\\le\\exp(5b^{0.56})\\quad(x\\in G_b),\\qquad\n \\left|\\mathbb E_z F_b(t_z)\\mathbf 1_{U_b}(z)-1\\right|\n       \\le A b^{-1/20}.\n \\label{eq:block-usable-bounds}$$ There is also a base version with no $H$, $g_0$, or $W$: take $U_b=E_b$ and choose $C_b$ so that the conclusions concerning $g_b$ in Equation <a href=\"#eq:block-usable-bounds\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"eq:block-usable-bounds\">[eq:block-usable-bounds]</a> hold.",
        "source_id": "PM-092.1"
      },
      "evidence": [

    {
      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-lem-block-cell",
        "exact_quote": "Let $L_b=\\mathbb Z^b+C_b/p_b$. For $t\\in\\mathbb R^b$ put $k=\\lfloor p_b t\\rfloor\\in\\mathbb Z^b$ and $x=k\\pmod{p_b}\\in G_b$. Floors and fractional parts are taken coordinatewise. If $g_b(x)>0$, choose $c\\in C_b$ attaining its maximum and let $z=x-c\\in U_b$, again using standard representatives. Then $$\\lambda=\\frac{k-z}{p_b}\\in L_b,\\qquad\n \\tau=t-\\lambda=\\frac{z+\\{p_b t\\}}{p_b}\\in Q_z,\\qquad\n g_b(x)=\\frac{F_b(t_z)}{p_b}.\n \\label{eq:block-cell-identity}$$ For all $\\varepsilon\\in\\{0,1\\}^b$, the point $\\lambda+\\varepsilon$ lies in $L_b$, its residual $t-\\lambda-\\varepsilon=\\tau-\\varepsilon$ has coordinates in $[-1,1]$, and $$\\frac{h_b^b}{p_b}\\gamma_b\\bigl(h_b(t-\\lambda-\\varepsilon)\\bigr)\n       \\ge e^{-\\eta_b}g_b(x)P_{b,t_z}(\\varepsilon).\n \\label{eq:block-cell-lower}$$ The corresponding individual weight is at most $e^{-c b^{0.70}}$.",

      },

<a id="pm-s2"></a>
## 2. Definitions, validity and bounds

- **lattice covering density** ([source](#sec:introduction)): For a convex body $K\subset\mathbb R^n$, its lattice covering density is $$\theta_L(K)=\inf\left\{ \frac{\operatorname{vol}_n(K)}{\det\Lambda}:\Lambda\text{ is a full-rank lattice and } K+\Lambda=\mathbb R^n\right\}.$$
- **convex body and lattice covolume** ([source](#sec:introduction)): Here a convex body is compact with nonempty interior, and $\det\Lambda$ denotes lattice covolume.
- **Gaussian density** ([source](#pm-h-6)): The standard Gaussian density is $$\gamma_d(y)=(2\pi)^{-d/2}\exp(-\\|y\\|^2/2).$$
- **isotropic law** ([source](#eq:gaussian-marginal-input)): Here isotropic means $\mathbb EX=0$ and $\mathbb EXX^{\mathsf T}=I_n$; orthonormal coordinates identify $E$ with $\mathbb R^D$.
- **hole proportion** ([source](#eq:mean-hole-input)): For a lattice $\Lambda\subset\mathbb R^m$, write $$h_{\Lambda}(J) =1-\|\pi_\Lambda(J)\|,$$ where the measure on $\mathbb R^m/\Lambda$ is normalized to be a probability measure.
- **binary by suffixes** ([source](#pm-h-22)): Call it *binary by suffixes* if, after fixing any suffix of coordinates, the next coordinate has at most two possible values, and two such values always differ by $1$.
- **raw coordinate lattice** ([source](#pm-h-15)): For $i\le r$, define the raw coordinate lattice $$L_i=\mathbb Z^{b_i}+p_i^{-1}C_i. \label{eq:hierarchy-raw-lattice}$$ Here the right side means the union of the integer translates of the standard representatives divided by $p_i$; it is independent of the choice of representatives. It is a lattice of determinant $p_i^{-1}$. Indeed, choose a generator $c$ of $C_i$ with its $j$th coordinate equal to $1$. The vector $c/p_i$ and the vectors $e_k$ for $k\ne j$ are a basis: they generate $e_j=p_i(c/p_i)-\sum_{k\ne j}c_k e_k$ and all of $L_i$, and their determinant has absolute value $1/p_i$. Adjoin a terminal block of size $$T=b_{r+1}=b_r\lceil\log_2 p_r\rceil, \qquad h_{r+1}=1,\qquad L_{r+1}=\mathbb Z^T.$$

          "exact_quote": "For a lattice $\\Lambda\\subset\\mathbb R^m$, write $$h_{\\Lambda}(J)\n =1-|\\pi_\\Lambda(J)|,$$ where the measure on $\\mathbb R^m/\\Lambda$ is normalized to be a probability measure.",
          "source_id": "PM-092.1"
        }
      ],
      "exact_definition": "For a lattice $\\Lambda\\subset\\mathbb R^m$, write $$h_{\\Lambda}(J)\n =1-|\\pi_\\Lambda(J)|,$$ where the measure on $\\mathbb R^m/\\Lambda$ is normalized to be a probability measure.",
      "interpretation": "The normalized torus fraction missed by the projected body.",
      "kind": "notation",

    {
      "definition_anchor": "pm-h-22",
      "evidence": [
        {
          "anchor": "pm-h-22",
          "exact_quote": "Call it *binary by suffixes* if, after fixing any suffix of coordinates, the next coordinate has at most two possible values, and two such values always differ by $1$.",
          "source_id": "PM-092.1"
        }
      ],
      "exact_definition": "Call it *binary by suffixes* if, after fixing any suffix of coordinates, the next coordinate has at most two possible values, and two such values always differ by $1$.",
      "interpretation": "The next coordinate has at most two values after fixing a suffix, with unit sibling separation.",
      "kind": "definition",
      "name": "binary by suffixes",
      "scope": "Finite labeled patterns for the simultaneous shear."
    },
    {
      "definition_anchor": "pm-h-15",
      "evidence": [
        {
          "anchor": "pm-h-15",
          "exact_quote": "For $i\\le r$, define the raw coordinate lattice $$L_i=\\mathbb Z^{b_i}+p_i^{-1}C_i.\n \\label{eq:hierarchy-raw-lattice}$$ Here the right side means the union of the integer translates of the standard representatives divided by $p_i$; it is independent of the choice of representatives. It is a lattice of determinant $p_i^{-1}$. Indeed, choose a generator $c$ of $C_i$ with its $j$th coordinate equal to $1$. The vector $c/p_i$ and the vectors $e_k$ for $k\\ne j$ are a basis: they generate $e_j=p_i(c/p_i)-\\sum_{k\\ne j}c_k e_k$ and all of $L_i$, and their determinant has absolute value $1/p_i$.\n\n\nAdjoin a terminal block of size $$T=b_{r+1}=b_r\\lceil\\log_2 p_r\\rceil,\n \\qquad h_{r+1}=1,\\qquad L_{r+1}=\\mathbb Z^T.$$",
          "source_id": "PM-092.1"
        }
      ],
      "exact_definition": "For $i\\le r$, define the raw coordinate lattice $$L_i=\\mathbb Z^{b_i}+p_i^{-1}C_i.\n \\label{eq:hierarchy-raw-lattice}$$ Here the right side means the union of the integer translates of the standard representatives divided by $p_i$; it is independent of the choice of representatives. It is a lattice of determinant $p_i^{-1}$. Indeed, choose a generator $c$ of $C_i$ with its $j$th coordinate equal to $1$. The vector $c/p_i$ and the vectors $e_k$ for $k\\ne j$ are a basis: they generate $e_j=p_i(c/p_i)-\\sum_{k\\ne j}c_k e_k$ and all of $L_i$, and their determinant has absolute value $1/p_i$.\n\n\nAdjoin a terminal block of size $$T=b_{r+1}=b_r\\lceil\\log_2 p_r\\rceil,\n \\qquad h_{r+1}=1,\\qquad L_{r+1}=\\mathbb Z^T.$$",
      "interpretation": "The integer lattice enlarged by a line subgroup divided by its prime modulus.",
      "kind": "definition",
      "name": "raw coordinate lattice",
      "scope": "Ordinary blocks of the hierarchy."
    }
  ],

Let $b\\ge b_0$, let $H$ be a finite abelian group written additively, and suppose that $$\\log |H|\\le b^4,\n  \\qquad 0\\le g\\le \\exp(b^{0.65}),\n  \\qquad \\bar g:=\\mathbb E_{x\\in H}g(x)\\in[1/2,2].$$ Let $s$ be an integer satisfying $b^{0.69}\\le s\\le b$, and choose $w_1,\\ldots,w_s$ independently and uniformly in $H$. With $c_*=(\\log 2)/8$, the probability that $$\\label{eq:sampler-conclusion}\n  2^{-s}\\sum_{\\varepsilon\\in\\{0,1\\}^s}\n  g\\left(x-\\sum_{i=1}^s\\varepsilon_iw_i\\right)\n  \\ge (1-3b^{-1/10})\\bar g\n  \\qquad\\text{for every }x\\in H$$ is at least $$\\label{eq:sampler-probability}\n  1-\\exp(-c_*b^{0.69})\n    -b\\exp\\left(-\\frac{c_*}{2}b^{0.59}\\right).$$ In particular, the failure probability is at most $b^{-1/2}$ after increasing $b_0$.",
            "source_id": "PM-092.1"
          }

            "exact_quote": "For every sufficiently large $b$, there is a prime $p_b$ satisfying $$b\\mu_b-4b^{0.56}\\le\\log p_b\\le b\\mu_b-3b^{0.56}.\n \\label{eq:block-prime}$$ Let $G_b=\\mathbb F_{p_b}^b$, and associate with $z\\in G_b$ the anchor $t_z=z/p_b\\in[0,1)^b$, using the representatives $0,\\ldots,p_b-1$ in every coordinate. Define the eligible set $E_b\\subset G_b$ by the two conditions $$\\left|\\log F_b(t_z)-b\\mu_b\\right|\\le b^{0.56},\\qquad\n \\#\\left\\{j:\\left|t_{z,j}-\\frac12\\right|\\le h_b^{-2}\\right\\}\n       \\ge b^{0.70}.\n \\label{eq:block-eligible}$$ Then $$\\mathbb E_{z\\in G_b}F_b(t_z)=1+O(b^{-1/20}),\\qquad\n \\mathbb E_{z\\in G_b}F_b(t_z)\\mathbf 1_{E_b}(z)=1+O(b^{-1/20}).\n \\label{eq:block-mass}$$ Moreover, $\\mu_b=\\log h_b+O(1)$. Put $\\eta_b=bh_b^2/p_b$. If $t$ belongs to the grid cell $Q_z=t_z+[0,p_b^{-1})^b$, then, for every $\\varepsilon\\in\\{0,1\\}^b$, $$\\begin{split}\n e^{-\\eta_b}F_b(t_z)&\\le F_b(t)\\le e^{\\eta_b}F_b(t_z),\\\\\n e^{-\\eta_b}\\frac{F_b(t_z)}{p_b}P_{b,t_z}(\\varepsilon)\n &\\le\\frac{h_b^b}{p_b}\\gamma_b\\bigl(h_b(t-\\varepsilon)\\bigr)\n \\le e^{\\eta_b}\\frac{F_b(t_z)}{p_b}P_{b,t_z}(\\varepsilon).\n \\end{split}\n \\label{eq:block-rounding}$$ There is an absolute $c>0$ such that, for every eligible $z$, every $t\\in Q_z$, and every $\\varepsilon\\in\\{0,1\\}^b$, $$\\frac{F_b(t_z)}{p_b}P_{b,t_z}(\\varepsilon)\\le e^{-c b^{0.70}},\n \\qquad\n \\frac{h_b^b}{p_b}\\gamma_b\\bigl(h_b(t-\\varepsilon)\\bigr)\n       \\le e^{-c b^{0.70}}.\n \\label{eq:block-atom}$$",
            "source_id": "PM-092.1"
          }
        ],
        "parameter_scope": "Absolute constants and thresholds are independent of the convex body; unspecified numerical values are ABSENT.",

            "exact_quote": "For every sufficiently large $b$, there is a prime $p_b$ satisfying $$b\\mu_b-4b^{0.56}\\le\\log p_b\\le b\\mu_b-3b^{0.56}.\n \\label{eq:block-prime}$$ Let $G_b=\\mathbb F_{p_b}^b$, and associate with $z\\in G_b$ the anchor $t_z=z/p_b\\in[0,1)^b$, using the representatives $0,\\ldots,p_b-1$ in every coordinate. Define the eligible set $E_b\\subset G_b$ by the two conditions $$\\left|\\log F_b(t_z)-b\\mu_b\\right|\\le b^{0.56},\\qquad\n \\#\\left\\{j:\\left|t_{z,j}-\\frac12\\right|\\le h_b^{-2}\\right\\}\n       \\ge b^{0.70}.\n \\label{eq:block-eligible}$$ Then $$\\mathbb E_{z\\in G_b}F_b(t_z)=1+O(b^{-1/20}),\\qquad\n \\mathbb E_{z\\in G_b}F_b(t_z)\\mathbf 1_{E_b}(z)=1+O(b^{-1/20}).\n \\label{eq:block-mass}$$ Moreover, $\\mu_b=\\log h_b+O(1)$. Put $\\eta_b=bh_b^2/p_b$. If $t$ belongs to the grid cell $Q_z=t_z+[0,p_b^{-1})^b$, then, for every $\\varepsilon\\in\\{0,1\\}^b$, $$\\begin{split}\n e^{-\\eta_b}F_b(t_z)&\\le F_b(t)\\le e^{\\eta_b}F_b(t_z),\\\\\n e^{-\\eta_b}\\frac{F_b(t_z)}{p_b}P_{b,t_z}(\\varepsilon)\n &\\le\\frac{h_b^b}{p_b}\\gamma_b\\bigl(h_b(t-\\varepsilon)\\bigr)\n \\le e^{\\eta_b}\\frac{F_b(t_z)}{p_b}P_{b,t_z}(\\varepsilon).\n \\end{split}\n \\label{eq:block-rounding}$$ There is an absolute $c>0$ such that, for every eligible $z$, every $t\\in Q_z$, and every $\\varepsilon\\in\\{0,1\\}^b$, $$\\frac{F_b(t_z)}{p_b}P_{b,t_z}(\\varepsilon)\\le e^{-c b^{0.70}},\n \\qquad\n \\frac{h_b^b}{p_b}\\gamma_b\\bigl(h_b(t-\\varepsilon)\\bigr)\n       \\le e^{-c b^{0.70}}.\n \\label{eq:block-atom}$$",
            "source_id": "PM-092.1"
          }
        ],

<a id="pm-s3"></a>
## 3. Proof roadmap

- **R1** [claim](#stmt-lem-sections) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-sections): Affine normalization and a finite section net. -> Finite horizontal section labels.
- **R2** [claim](#stmt-lem-horizontal) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-horizontal): Mean-hole estimate, Markov inequality, and a finite union bound. -> A common horizontal lattice.
- **R3** [claim](#stmt-lem-sampler) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-sampler): Second moment control followed by exponential-potential iteration. -> Uniform Boolean sampling.
- **R4** [claim](#stmt-lem-folded-grid) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-folded-grid): Gaussian folding, grid rounding, and balanced-coordinate control. -> Eligible anchors with retained mass and small atoms.
- **R5** [claim](#stmt-lem-usable) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-usable): Fair-bit mixtures and weighted line-collision control. -> Usable anchors and a line coset maximum.
- **R6** [claim](#stmt-lem-block-cell) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-block-cell): Choose a maximizing anchor and apply grid rounding. -> Lattice cell points and Gaussian weight comparisons.
- **R7** [claim](#stmt-prop-vertical) / [proof](#proof-stmt-prop-vertical): Retain weighted branches through the block hierarchy and apply terminal correction. -> Vertical patterns with positive total mass and small atoms.
- **R8** [claim](#stmt-lem-shear) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-shear): Induction over suffix slices and a probabilistic choice of relative shifts. -> A common linear shear.
- **R9** [claim](#stmt-lem-completion) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-completion): Torus measure comparison and convex Minkowski addition. -> Exact coverage after dilation.
- **R10** [claim](#stmt-thm-main) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-completion-2): Combine horizontal loads, a common shear, completion by dilation, and affine invariance; handle remaining dimensions by a maximal simplex. -> A single covering lattice with the stated density bound.

<a id="pm-s4"></a>
## 4. Engineering Triptych

### Panel 1 - epistemic contract

- **I-cover**: Every convex body admits a covering by a full-rank lattice with density bounded in terms of the dimension.
- **I-terminal**: The terminal binary columns exhaust the last finite group.
- **I-mass**: Total pattern weight and individual atom weight are separate requirements.

### Panel 2 - negative knowledge

- **A-nonconvex**: Apply the density guarantee to an arbitrary footprint. -> A nonconvex or unbounded footprint need not satisfy the theorem.
- **A-effective**: Treat the density bound as an explicit lattice synthesis algorithm. -> Absolute constants and asymptotic thresholds are not supplied as numerical inputs.
- **A-branch**: Keep only a successful branch and claim the same load. -> An individual atom bound cannot replace the retained total weight.

### Panel 3 - reference implementation and oracle

Applicability: `constructive`. The terminal binary-column exhaustion is finite and constructive. This implementation checks that component only; it does not construct the covering lattice or certify the asymptotic density bound.

```python
# INPUTS: JSON object with integer p and b.
# PRECONDITIONS: prime 2 <= p <= 31; 1 <= b <= 2.
# OUTPUTS: all targets in the finite group and binary column witnesses, or an error.
# TIME: O(p**b * b * ceil(log2(p))) exact integer operations.
# SPACE: O(p**b * b * ceil(log2(p))) output bits.
# ARITHMETIC: exact fmpz integer division, remainder and primality.
# FAILURE: invalid bounded inputs return an error; no global covering certificate.
import json, sys
from itertools import product
from flint import fmpz
params = json.loads(sys.stdin.read())
p, b = params.get('p'), params.get('b')
valid = type(p) is int and type(b) is int and 2 <= p <= 31 and 1 <= b <= 2
if not valid or not fmpz(p).is_prime():
    print(json.dumps({'error': 'precondition'}))
else:
    width = int(fmpz(p - 1).bit_length())
    rows = []
    for target in product(range(p), repeat=b):
        bits = []
        for coordinate in target:
            quotient = fmpz(coordinate)
            for k in range(width):
                bits.append(int(quotient % 2))
                quotient = quotient // 2
        rows.append({'target': list(target), 'bits': bits})
    print(json.dumps({'width': width, 'rows': rows}, separators=(',', ':')))
```

            "exact_quote": "These two properties play different roles: the total mass supplies enough horizontal load, and the atom bound keeps every section within the range of the mean-hole estimate.",

        "id": "A-branch",

  "panel3": {
    "applicability": "constructive",
    "coverage_map": [
      {
        "checked_domain": "Every target for p in {2,3,5,7,31}, b in {1,2}; every scalar Boolean label at these primes. Invalid cases p=1,4,32 or b=0,3 are rejected.",
        "coverage": "PARTIAL",
        "invariant_id": "I-terminal",
        "oracle_basis": "Independent exhaustive Boolean subset-sum image and native exact integer witness reconstruction; prime check independent of fmpz. The asymptotic covering theorem is outside this check."
      }
    ],
    "execution_status": "PASS",
    "host_receipt_sha256": "70e8d257361d75fb505929bf4d2a5e6771c631787a9a751f5989f026edeaa204",
    "independent_reference": "The terminal construction defines columns as powers of two times coordinate vectors and asserts that their Boolean image exhausts the finite group.",
    "input_contract": "Bounded terminal component: prime integer p with 2 <= p <= 31 and integer block dimension 1 <= b <= 2; JSON p,b. Invalid bounded inputs return an error.",
    "language": "python",
    "mutants": [
      {
        "expected_failure": "The witness dimension or reconstructed target differs.",
        "id": "M1",
        "mutation": "Drop the highest binary column.",
        "observed_exit": 1,
        "receipt_sha256": "eef2746d2db7a52f48e1cdfc1ec1938ae628b5fcf1d4b8d7a5e51571ac3c934b"
      },
      {
        "expected_failure": "Exact reconstruction rejects a changed target.",
        "id": "M2",
        "mutation": "Reverse each bit value.",
        "observed_exit": 1,
        "receipt_sha256": "1eb2766b4d871c7d28919ae63217b026828f968d5061d509a480322846d4eee3"
      },
      {
        "expected_failure": "The target set is not exhaustive.",
        "id": "M3",
        "mutation": "Omit the last representative in every coordinate.",
        "observed_exit": 1,
        "receipt_sha256": "47f8c0f04e52c53ceeeec34d869a19462162626fe51cfde5636954f7ec74dcce"
      },
      {
        "expected_failure": "A composite input must return the precondition error.",
        "id": "M4",
        "mutation": "Accept composite moduli.",
        "observed_exit": 1,
        "receipt_sha256": "f148a6681a42df9f910338580b61156d7416aa5944ad56d85d35732579dcb473"
      }
    ],
    "oracle_code": "import sys, json, itertools, math\nfrom sympy import isprime\nargs = json.load(open(sys.argv[1]))\nprime = args.get('p'); dim = args.get('b')\nanswer = json.load(open(sys.argv[2]))\nclaimed = json.loads(sys.argv[3])\nallowed = type(prime) is int and type(dim) is int and 2 <= prime <= 31 and 1 <= dim <= 2 and isprime(prime)\nif not allowed:\n    assert answer == claimed == {'error': 'precondition'}\n    sys.exit(0)\nlength = 0\nwhile 2**length < prime:\n    length += 1\nassert answer.keys() == {'width', 'rows'} and answer['width'] == length\n# An independent exhaustive image calculation over every Boolean label.\nimages = {sum(bit * 2**index for index, bit in enumerate(label)) % prime\n          for label in itertools.product((0, 1), repeat=length)}\nassert images == set(range(prime))\nseen = set()\nassert len(answer['rows']) == prime**dim\nfor witness in answer['rows']:\n    assert witness.keys() == {'target', 'bits'}\n    point, label = witness['target'], witness['bits']\n    assert len(point) == dim and all(type(x) is int and 0 <= x < prime for x in point)\n    assert len(label) == length*dim and all(type(y) is int and y in (0, 1) for y in label)\n    decoded = [sum(label[j*length+k] * pow(2, k) for k in range(length)) for j in range(dim)]\n    assert decoded == point\n    assert tuple(point) not in seen\n    seen.add(tuple(point))\nassert seen == set(itertools.product(images, repeat=dim))\nassert answer == claimed\n",
    "output_contract": "A binary witness for every group target, with coordinate columns ordered by increasing powers of two; invalid inputs return a precondition error.",
    "reason": "The terminal binary-column exhaustion is finite and constructive. This implementation checks that component only; it does not construct the covering lattice or certify the asymptotic density bound.",
    "reference_code": "# INPUTS: JSON object with integer p and b.\n# PRECONDITIONS: prime 2 <= p <= 31; 1 <= b <= 2.\n# OUTPUTS: all targets in the finite group and binary column witnesses, or an error.\n# TIME: O(p**b * b * ceil(log2(p))) exact integer operations.\n# SPACE: O(p**b * b * ceil(log2(p))) output bits.\n# ARITHMETIC: exact fmpz integer division, remainder and primality.\n# FAILURE: invalid bounded inputs return an error; no global covering certificate.\nimport json, sys\nfrom itertools import product\nfrom flint import fmpz\nparams = json.loads(sys.stdin.read())\np, b = params.get('p'), params.get('b')\nvalid = type(p) is int and type(b) is int and 2 <= p <= 31 and 1 <= b <= 2\nif not valid or not fmpz(p).is_prime():\n    print(json.dumps({'error': 'precondition'}))\nelse:\n    width = int(fmpz(p - 1).bit_length())\n    rows = []\n    for target in product(range(p), repeat=b):\n        bits = []\n        for coordinate in target:\n            quotient = fmpz(coordinate)\n            for k in range(width):\n                bits.append(int(quotient % 2))\n                quotient = quotient // 2\n        rows.append({'target': list(target), 'bits': bits})\n    print(json.dumps({'width': width, 'rows': rows}, separators=(',', ':')))\n",

<a id="pm-s5"></a>
## 5. Domain engineering contracts

| domain | applies | strength | maturity | basis | why |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| software | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | No representation of software states as convex-body translates is established. The covering theorem alone supplies no software correctness or execution guarantee. |
| games_simulation | True | weak | inspirational | inferred | A continuous placement model can use the existence bound conditionally when a convex footprint is translated along a full-rank lattice. Obstacles and nonconvex playable regions require separate analysis. |
| tools | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | The paper establishes geometric and probabilistic statements but no tool interface or numerical certification procedure. A direct tool contract is ABSENT. |
| algorithms | True | moderate | adaptable | inferred | The uniform Boolean sampler conditionally provides a randomized finite-group averaging primitive. Its simultaneous lower bound can accommodate base points chosen after the shifts, provided the stated group, function, size, and cutoff hypotheses hold. |
| data_structures | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | Finite groups and coordinate alphabets organize the proof, but no storage, lookup, update, or indexing contract is derived. |
| hardware_accelerators | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | The source gives no mapping from a lattice cover to accelerator execution, memory traffic, or physical hardware placement constraints. |
| crypto_security | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | A lattice covering upper bound is a geometric existence statement. It establishes no computational hardness or security reduction. |
| machine_learning | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | The Gaussian weights support a geometric covering proof; no statistical learning model or optimisation guarantee is established. |
| optimisation_operations_research | True | moderate | inspirational | inferred | Geometric placement with a convex footprint and unrestricted full-rank lattice centers can use the universal density bound as an existence benchmark. A budgeted or obstacle-constrained placement model requires a separate reduction. |
| distributed_systems | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | No agreement protocol, fault model, or distributed state transition system is related to the convex-body covering model. |
| other (geometric_coverage) | True | strong | inspirational | stated | In geometric coverage, the theorem directly supplies a single-lattice covering existence bound for every convex body in the stated ambient dimensions, without symmetry or boundary regularity. Practical construction and verification remain separate obligations. |

<a id="pm-s6"></a>
## 6. Applicability limits and search

- **L-convex-body** (hypothesis): The covering theorem requires a compact convex body with nonempty interior. Nonconvex, unbounded, or lower-dimensional sets require a separate reduction or result. Blocked use: ['Direct coverage guarantees for sets that do not satisfy the convex-body assumptions.']
- **L-dimension** (edge_case): The theorem concerns integer dimensions $n\ge2$. Its statement does not supply a guarantee outside that dimension range. Blocked use: ['Applying the stated theorem outside its dimension range.']
- **L-center-constraint** (hypothesis): The guarantee uses a full-rank lattice of translation centers. A translative construction allowing multiple lattice cosets does not by itself establish the single-lattice result. Blocked use: ['Replacing the single-lattice proof by a multiple-coset translative bound.']
- **L-density** (counterexample_scope): Density measures average overlap in a fundamental cell. It does not alone bound pointwise multiplicity, finite-region center counts, boundary overhead, or admissible-center constraints. Blocked use: ['Using an average density as a pointwise overlap cap or an exact finite deployment count.']
- **L-worst-case** (counterexample_scope): The matching order of growth concerns the supremum over convex bodies. It does not assert that every individual body needs the worst-case density. Blocked use: ['Assigning a worst-case lower bound to every convex body.']
- **L-thresholds** (unknown): Numerical values for the absolute constants and asymptotic cutoffs are ABSENT. Sufficiently-large regimes cannot be converted into explicit deployment thresholds from the stated bounds alone. Blocked use: ['Claiming a numerical universal density constant or an explicit cutoff without additional evidence.']
- **L-existence** (nonconstructive): The main theorem is an existence guarantee. The supplied proof does not specify a complete executable lattice constructor with a certified runtime for an arbitrary input body; that contract is ABSENT. Finite subcomponents can be implemented without certifying the entire covering construction. Blocked use: ['Treating a finite component test as a certified construction of the covering lattice for arbitrary convex bodies.']
- **L-sampler** (hypothesis): The uniform sampler requires a finite abelian group, independent uniform shifts, and the stated size, function-cap, mean, and cutoff hypotheses. A successful average at most base points does not establish its simultaneous conclusion. Blocked use: ['Applying the sampler without its hypotheses or substituting an average over base points for a uniform guarantee.']
- **L-sparse-cosets** (edge_case): The coset-maximum function may vanish at targets. Its near-unit mean must be recovered by the next sampling stage; it is not a pointwise positive coverage certificate. Blocked use: ['Assuming every coset has a usable anchor or selecting one successful branch instead of retaining total weighted load.']
- **L-shear** (hypothesis): The simultaneous shear result requires finite labeled pattern families closed under last-coordinate slicing and binary by suffixes. Arbitrary branching patterns are outside its stated scope. Blocked use: ['Using the shear bound for patterns lacking the suffix rule or slicing closure.']
- **L-completion** (approximation): A small uncovered fraction is not exact coverage. Completion requires the strict hole inequality and then dilates the convex body; preserving density requires the corresponding lattice rescaling. Blocked use: ['Calling an almost-covering configuration exact without satisfying completion and accounting for dilation.']
- **L-formal** (formal_scope): A machine-checked proof of the full covering theorem is ABSENT from the supplied evidence. Exact finite checks of auxiliary identities do not establish all geometric and asymptotic arguments. Blocked use: ['Publishing finite arithmetic checks as formal verification of the full theorem.']

### Formal statement (Lean 4)

Corpus status: formalized in the source corpus (toolchain `leanprover/lean4:v4.34.1`, Mathlib `d13f23b723b8a846827a245b89c10fc7d3f11612`). Not re-checked here; the statements give the exact quantifiers and hypotheses, not a proof.

Scope note of family 092 (`lean/docs/092.md`, verbatim):

> The following describes the scope of the Lean formalization related to the following accompanying paper(s):
>
> - [A single-lattice covering bound of order $n\log n$](../../preprints/A-single-lattice-covering-bound-of-order-n-log-n-September-23-2026/paper.pdf)
> - [Translative covering densities of order $n\log n$](../../preprints/Translative-covering-densities-of-order-n-log-n-September-23-2026/paper.pdf)
>
> ## Scope
>
> The formalized result gives an absolute constant $C>0$ such that every convex body $K\subset\mathbb R^n$, $n\ge2$, admits a covering by translates along one full-rank lattice $L$ with density $|K|/\mathrm{covol}(L)\le Cn\log n$. The covering is exact, and no symmetry, boundary regularity, or volume normalization is assumed.
>
> The formalization determines the order of the largest covering density in dimension $n$. For all sufficiently large $n$, the suprema of translative covering density and lattice covering density are each bounded above and below by absolute positive multiples of $n\log n$, both over all convex bodies and over centrally symmetric convex bodies.

Comparator source `SingleLatticeCovering.lean` (874 bytes, sha256 `f2f6684141cab6c648c934f75685a3d85f12f37f3c2b0c83224155157a60dbcd`), verbatim:

```lean
import Mathlib

namespace OAI

/-! Convex bodies and exact coverings by a single lattice in real coordinate space. -/

namespace SingleLatticeCovering

open MeasureTheory
open scoped Pointwise

abbrev Space (n : ℕ) := Fin n → ℝ

def IsConvexBody {n : ℕ} (K : Set (Space n)) : Prop :=
  IsCompact K ∧ Convex ℝ K ∧ (interior K).Nonempty

def LatticeCovers {n : ℕ} (K : Set (Space n)) (L : Submodule ℤ (Space n)) : Prop :=
  K + (L : Set (Space n)) = Set.univ

theorem single_lattice_covering :
    ∃ C : ℝ, 0 < C ∧ ∀ (n : ℕ), 2 ≤ n →
      ∀ (K : Set (Space n)), IsConvexBody K →
        ∃ (L : Submodule ℤ (Space n)) (_ : DiscreteTopology L),
          IsZLattice ℝ L ∧ LatticeCovers K L ∧
            (volume K).toReal / ZLattice.covolume L ≤ C * (n : ℝ) * Real.log (n : ℝ) := by sorry

end SingleLatticeCovering

end OAI
```

<a id="pm-s7"></a>
## 7. Complete mathematical paper


Every convex body in ℝ<sup><i>n</i></sup>, <i>n</i> ≥ 2, admits a covering by translates along one full-rank lattice with density at most $`Cn\log n`$, for an absolute constant <i>C</i>. No symmetry or boundary regularity is assumed.

<a id="pm-h-1"></a>
#### Statements index

| [stmt-lem-sections](#stmt-lem-sections) | lemma | `lem:sections` | 2 | There are absolute constants $c,C>0$ with the following property. |
| [stmt-lem-horizontal](#stmt-lem-horizontal) | lemma | `lem:horizontal` | 3 | Let $J_1,\ldots,J_N$ be convex bodies in $\mathbb R^m$, let $D_h>0$, and set $u_a=\operatorname{vol}_m(J_a)/D_h$. |
| [stmt-lem-sampler](#stmt-lem-sampler) | lemma | `lem:sampler` | 4 | There is an absolute constant $b_0$ with the following property. |
| [stmt-auto-remark-1](#stmt-auto-remark-1) | remark | - | - | Remark 5* (Uniformity and offsets). |
